Gemini 3.6 FlashProduction7.5

Deere & Company reported fiscal Q3 2026 net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion, up 5% year-over-year, with Equipment Operations operating margin reaching 14.4%. Net income attributable to Deere & Company reached $1.379 billion, or $5.10 per diluted share. Full-year FY 2026 net income guidance was raised to a range of $4.75 billion to $5.00 billion, driven by strong early order program intake and lower direct tariff expenses.

Key takeaways

  • Deanna Kovar, President of Worldwide Ag & Turf, stated that early order program results reinforce management's view that fiscal 2026 represents the bottom of the agricultural equipment cycle.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, reported that FY 2026 direct tariff expense expectations dropped to $1.1 billion from $1.2 billion due to Section 232 European rate cuts from 25% to 15%.
  • Brent Norwood, CFO, highlighted that net tariff expense will increase to approximately $1.0 billion in FY 2027 from roughly $750 million in FY 2026.
  • Early order programs for planters and sprayers are up mid-single digits, supported by factory orders for See & Spray doubling year-over-year.

Key metrics

  • Net Sales and Revenues $12.608 billion up 5% year-over-year
  • Equipment Operations Net Sales $10.999 billion up 6% year-over-year
  • Equipment Operations Operating Margin 14.4% for Q3 FY 2026
  • Net Income Attributable to Deere & Company $1.379 billion or $5.10 per diluted share
  • Construction & Forestry Net Sales $3.618 billion up 18% year-over-year
  • Small Ag & Turf Net Sales $3.383 billion up 12% year-over-year
  • Production & Precision Ag Net Sales $3.998 billion down 6% year-over-year
  • Engaged Acres in Operations Center >520 million acres across nearly 1.2 million connected machines

Financial highlights

  • Equipment Operations net sales grew 6% year-over-year to $10.999 billion with an operating margin of 14.4%.
  • Construction & Forestry net sales surged 18% year-over-year to $3.618 billion, delivering an operating profit of $436 million and price realization of +8 points.
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales rose 12% year-over-year to $3.383 billion with an 18.4% operating margin.
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales declined 6% year-over-year to $3.998 billion, generating $527 million in operating profit at a 13.2% operating margin.

Guidance

  • Dan Pooley, Manager of Investor Communications, stated FY 2026 net income guidance was raised to $4.75 billion-$5.00 billion.
  • Dan Pooley projected FY 2026 Equipment Operations cash flows of $5.0 billion-$5.5 billion, up from the prior range.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, updated FY 2026 direct tariff expense to $1.1 billion, down from $1.2 billion previously.
  • Brent Norwood, CFO, anticipated FY 2027 net tariff expense to rise to approximately $1.0 billion from roughly $750 million in FY 2026.
  • Dan Pooley detailed full-year industry sales expectations of down 15%-20% for U.S. & Canada Large Ag and up 5%-10% for U.S. & Canada Earthmoving Construction.

Q&A

  • Jamie Cook, Truist Securities: How is management thinking about production versus retail setup by region for 2027, and what are the pricing expectations given input inflation?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Deere is underproducing retail demand in 2026 by a couple of percentage points, and model year 2027 early order program pricing fully covers cost inflation across the PPA portfolio.
    answered directly
  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: What drove the lower FY 2026 direct tariff expense expectation of $1.1 billion versus $1.2 billion previously, and what is the update on the excavator launch?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Direct tariff reductions stem from Section 232 European rate drops from 25% to 15% on June 1st, while three Deere-designed excavators launched in spring with more models planned over 3-4 years.
    answered directly
  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did shipments pull forward into Q3 from Q4, and how should investors bridge performance from Q3 to Q4?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Some Q4 demand pulled into Q3 to mitigate execution risk, with Q4 net sales for PPA and C&F expected similar to Q3, though margins will step down due to seasonal R&D and SA&G spending.
    answered directly
  • Steve Fisher, UBS: Will net tariff impact be a headwind or tailwind in fiscal year 2027 compared to 2026?
    Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer: Net tariff expense will act as a headwind in FY 2027, increasing to approximately $1.0 billion from roughly $750 million in FY 2026.
    answered directly
  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How should we think about the potential benefit of product mix and high-technology sales on 2027 performance?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Management declined to quantify 2027 mix benefits given macroeconomic volatility in input costs and soft conditions in Europe and Brazil.
    declined to quantify
  • Jerry Revich, Wells Fargo: What regional variability are you seeing in the early order programs, and where could intake finish relative to current mid-single-digit growth?
    Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division: U.S. intake is trending slightly better than Canada, with See & Spray orders doubling and 40% of planters featuring advanced tech like ExactRate and FurrowVision.
    answered partially

Unanswered

  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How should we think about the potential benefit of product mix and high-technology sales on 2027 performance? Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, explicitly declined to quantify 2027 mix benefits due to macroeconomic input cost volatility.
  • Jerry Revich, Wells Fargo: Where could early order program intake finish relative to current mid-single-digit growth? Deanna Kovar, President of Worldwide Ag & Turf, detailed high precision technology adoption rates but omitted final order intake totals or target figures.
  • Peter Kalemkerian, Baird: Could upcoming European CAP budget policy changes in 2028 pull demand forward into 2027 or delay equipment purchases? Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, stated it was too early to determine policy impacts as Q1 order intake was just commencing.

Notable quotes

  • “John Deere delivered a strong third quarter, with equipment operations achieving 14.4% operating margin.” Dan Pooley, Manager, Investor Communications
  • “Overall, we view the early order program results as an encouraging signal that reinforces our view that 2026 represents the bottom of the agricultural equipment cycle.” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division
  • “We now have more than 520 million engaged acres across nearly 1.2 million connected machines.” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division
  • “John Deere has always supported our customers' ability to repair their own equipment for themselves or use whatever third party they trust the most.” Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer

What changed

  • Guidance: FY 2026 Net Income guidance raised to $4.75B-$5.00B from $4.5B-$5.0B.
  • Guidance: FY 2026 Equipment Operations Cash Flow raised to $5.0B-$5.5B from $4.5B-$5.5B.
  • Guidance: FY 2026 Direct Tariff Expense decreased to $1.1B from $1.2B ($750M net vs $900M net prior).
  • Guidance: Production & Precision Ag Net Sales guidance updated to down approx 10% from down 5%-10%.
  • Guidance: Production & Precision Ag Operating Margin narrowed to 11.0%-12.0% from 11%-13%.
  • Guidance: Small Ag & Turf Operating Margin increased to 14.5%-15.5% from 13.5%-15%.
  • Guidance: Construction & Forestry Price Realization increased to +3 points from 2.5 points.
  • Guidance: South America Ag Industry Sales outlook lowered to down 15%-20% from down about 15%.
  • New mentions: Section 301 tariffs, Operations Center PRO Service, Agriculture Financing program, Deere-designed excavators, ExactRate, ExactShot
  • Dropped: Casa John Deere, ExactDepth, See & Scout, JDLink Boost, Kernersville, North Carolina expansion, 8R / 8RX 440, 490, 540 HP tractors, Renewable volume obligations (RVO), Year-round E15
  • Tone: Management expressed greater conviction in operational stability, raising net income guidance and reiterating that fiscal 2026 marks the trough of the agricultural cycle.

Risks mentioned

  • European arable farmers face ongoing income headwinds from elevated input costs and heat stress.
  • Net tariff expense is projected to widen to approximately $1.0 billion in FY 2027 from roughly $750 million in FY 2026.
  • Q4 operating margins in Production & Precision Ag will step down seasonally due to higher R&D and SA&G spending alongside the absence of tariff refunds.
  • Growers in North America and Brazil continue to contend with elevated input prices that complicate application strategies.

Gemini 3.8 Flash7.5

Deere & Company reported fiscal Q3 2026 net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion, up 5% year-over-year, and net income of $1.379 billion. Performance was supported by $110 million in incremental IEPA tariff refunds during the quarter, bringing year-to-date refunds to $382 million. Based on factory execution and reduced Section 232 tariff rates, Deere narrowed and raised its full-year fiscal 2026 enterprise net income outlook to $4.75 billion to $5.0 billion.

Key takeaways

  • Deanna Kovar, President of Worldwide Ag & Turf, affirmed that early order program intake supports 2026 as the bottom of the ag equipment cycle, but pointed toward a measured recovery rather than a sharp rebound in 2027.
  • Brent Norwood, CFO, noted that direct tariff expense will drop to $1.1 billion in fiscal 2026 due to Section 232 rates decreasing to 15%, but net tariffs will be an incremental headwind in fiscal 2027 as refund activity ends.
  • Early order program planter and sprayer intake is tracking up mid-single digits, with factory-installed See & Spray orders doubling to roughly one-third of North American sprayers.
  • Construction & Forestry demand remained strong with backlogs at four to five months, driven by infrastructure, data centers, and expanding dealer rental fleets where 30% to 35% of earthmoving transactions now start as rentals.
  • Some tractor demand was pulled ahead into Q3 to manage Q4 execution risks, and Q4 segment margins will see seasonal pressure alongside an absence of further tariff refunds.

Key metrics

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608 billion up 5% year-over-year
  • Equipment operations net sales $10.999 billion up 6% year-over-year
  • Equipment operations operating margin 14.4% for the quarter
  • Net income attributable to Deere & Company $1.379 billion for the quarter
  • Diluted earnings per share $5.10 for the quarter
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales $3.998 billion down 6% year-over-year
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales $3.383 billion up 12% year-over-year
  • Construction & Forestry net sales $3.618 billion up 18% year-over-year

Financial highlights

  • Net sales and revenues increased 5% year-over-year to $12.608 billion, and diluted earnings per share was $5.10.
  • Equipment operations net sales rose 6% year-over-year to $10.999 billion, achieving an operating margin of 14.4%.
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales fell 6% year-over-year to $3.998 billion with an operating margin of 13.2% and operating profit of $527 million.
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales increased 12% to $3.383 billion with an operating profit of $622 million and an 18.4% operating margin.
  • Construction & Forestry net sales rose 18% to $3.618 billion, generating $436 million in operating profit at a 12.1% operating margin, aided by 8 points of price realization.

Guidance

  • Dan Pooley, Manager of Investor Communications, updated Deere & Company full-year net income guidance to $4.75 billion-$5.0 billion and equipment operating cash flows to $5.0 billion-$5.5 billion.
  • Dan Pooley, Manager of Investor Communications, guided Production & Precision Ag net sales down approximately 10% with operating margins between 11% and 12%.
  • Dan Pooley, Manager of Investor Communications, projected Small Ag & Turf net sales up approximately 15% with operating margins raised to 14.5%-15.5%.
  • Dan Pooley, Manager of Investor Communications, guided Construction & Forestry net sales up approximately 20% with operating margins between 10.5% and 11.5% and price realization raised to +3 points.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, expects full-year direct tariff expense to be approximately $1.1 billion, while Brent Norwood, CFO, projected direct tariffs of roughly $1 billion in fiscal 2027.

Q&A

  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: What is driving the reduction in expected direct tariff impact to $1.1 billion, and what is the status of the excavator launch and adoption timeline?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Direct tariff expense was lowered from $1.2 billion to $1.1 billion because Section 232 import tariff rates fell from roughly 25% to 15% effective June 1, creating a 5-month benefit in 2026 and an additional tailwind in 2027, while the first three Deere-designed excavators launched this spring with more rolling out over three to four years.
    answered directly
  • Steve Fisher, UBS: Will net tariff expense be a headwind or tailwind in fiscal 2027 compared to 2026?
    Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer: Net tariffs will be a headwind in fiscal 2027, as net tariff expense for fiscal 2026 is approximately $750 million ($1.1 billion direct tariffs less $382 million refunds) against an anticipated run rate of roughly $1 billion in 2027.
    answered directly
  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How could mix and higher precision technology adoption influence 2027 results compared to historical 2-3 point mix benefits?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Broader uncertainty in ag inputs, commodities, and recent softness in the EU and Brazil make it too early to quantify net mix impact, which will ultimately depend on final EOP wrap-up and combine order intake.
    answered partially
  • Chad Dillard, Bernstein: What dynamics drive the implied deceleration in C&F pricing in Q4, and how will expanding dealer rental fleets affect business economics?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Q3 C&F price realization of 8% benefited from lapping a negative 5% comparison in Q3 2025 caused by retail incentives, bringing full-year price guidance to 3%, while 30% to 35% of earthmoving transactions currently begin as rentals.
    answered directly
  • Peter Kalemkerian, Baird: Could the 2028 CAP budget policy change pull forward European equipment demand into 2027 or cause purchasing delays?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Management stated it is too early to determine if the 2028 CAP policy will pull forward or delay demand, pointing instead to stable European dairy and livestock sentiment and current order books of four to five months.
    declined to quantify

Unanswered

  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How will mix and precision ag technology adoption impact fiscal 2027 compared to the standard 2 to 3 points of benefit? Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, answered generally that it is too early to tell given commodity input uncertainty and ongoing EOP progress.
  • Peter Kalemkerian, Baird: Whether the 2028 CAP budget policy change in Europe will pull forward demand into 2027 or defer purchases. Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, explicitly declined to evaluate the timing impact, stating it is too early to determine.

Notable quotes

  • “Overall, we view the early order program results as an encouraging signal that reinforces our view that 2026 represents the bottom of the agricultural equipment cycle.” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division, Production & Precision Ag, Sales and Marketing, Regions of the Americas and Australia
  • “At the same time, the underlying fundamentals continue to support a measured recovery rather than a sharp rebound in 2027.” Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division, Production & Precision Ag, Sales and Marketing, Regions of the Americas and Australia
  • “As you think about our tariff expense this year versus next year, net tariffs, so direct tariffs paid less any refunds, will be a headwind going into next year.” Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer
  • “Today, anywhere from 30%-35% of earthmoving transactions start as a rental.” Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer
  • “Notably, our current outlook assumes no further refund activity during the balance of the fiscal year.” Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations

What changed

  • Guidance: Deere & Company net income guidance revised from $4.5B-$5.0B to $4.75B-$5.0B.
  • Guidance: Equipment operations operating cash flows raised from $4.5B-$5.5B to $5.0B-$5.5B.
  • Guidance: Direct tariff expense (excluding refunds) reduced from $1.2B to approximately $1.1B.
  • Guidance: Production & Precision Ag net sales narrowed from down 5%-10% to down approximately 10%, with operating margin adjusted from 11%-13% to 11%-12%.
  • Guidance: Small Ag & Turf operating margin raised from 13.5%-15% to 14.5%-15.5%.
  • Guidance: Construction & Forestry price realization raised from +2.5 points to +3 points, with operating margin revised from 10%-12% to 10.5%-11.5%.
  • Guidance: Financial Services net income increased from $860M to $870M.
  • Guidance: South America Ag & Turf industry sales guidance lowered from down 15% to down 15%-20%, and Europe Ag & Turf revised from flat to up 5% to approximately flat.
  • Guidance: Global Forestry industry sales worsened from down 5% to down 10%, while US & Canada Earthmoving improved from up around 5% to up 5%-10%.
  • New mentions: Phase II IEPA refunds, Section 301 tariffs, SmartGrade, Move Agriculture Financing program, ExactEmerge Planters, ExactShot, John Deere Operations Center PRO Service, FTC right-to-repair settlement
  • Dropped: JDLink Boost kits, Kernersville manufacturing facility, See & Scout, Casa John Deere, Share repurchases and capital returned to shareholders
  • Tone: The tone shifted from assessing used equipment inventory destocking to cautious optimism around early order program indicators confirming an ag trough. Management tempered expectations for fiscal 2027 by explicitly framing net tariffs as an upcoming headwind and emphasizing that the ag market recovery will be measured rather than sharp.

Risks mentioned

  • Net tariff headwinds in fiscal 2027 of roughly $1 billion as one-time IEPA refunds conclude.
  • Depressed farmer sentiment and margin pressure across Europe and Brazil driven by elevated input costs, weather stress, and currency dynamics.
  • South America Ag and Turf industry sales deterioration, guided down 15% to 20%.
  • Seasonal margin headwinds and lack of tariff refunds entering Q4 2026 alongside elevated R&D and SA&G spending.

Deere reported fiscal Q3 2026 net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion, up 5% year-over-year, alongside net income attributable to Deere & Company of $1.379 billion and diluted EPS of $5.10. Production and Precision Ag net sales declined 6% to $3.998 billion, while Small Ag & Turf and Construction & Forestry grew 12% and 18%, respectively. Management raised full-year guidance in several segments and cited strong precision technology adoption and positive order program trends.

Key takeaways

  • Production & Precision Ag and Construction & Forestry are experiencing modest underproduction of a couple percentage points this year due to caution in South America and strong order positions.
  • Early Order Programs for U.S. and regional large ag are trending up mid-single digits, supported by strong technology take rates such as a doubling of See & Spray usage and 40% adoption of advanced planter technologies.
  • Construction & Forestry demand remains very positive with four to five months of orders on hand, driven by large infrastructure, data centers, and independent rental channels.
  • Tariff expectations changed with Section 232 tariff rates dropping from 25% to 15% on imported goods effective June 1st, lowering the full-year direct tariff run rate to $1.1 billion.

Key metrics

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608 billion up 5%
  • Equipment operations net sales $10.999 billion up 6%
  • Production & Precision Ag net sales $3.998 billion down 6%
  • Small Ag & Turf net sales $3.383 billion up 12%
  • Construction & Forestry net sales $3.618 billion up 18%
  • Equipment operations operating margin 14.4%
  • Financial Services net income $219 million higher in the quarter

Q&A

  • Jamie Cook, Truist Securities: How are we thinking about production versus retail by region for 2027 and what are the pricing expectations given inflationary cost concerns?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: PPA and C&F are seeing modest underproduction of a couple percentage points this year due to South American caution and strong orders, with pricing focused on covering inflation across EOP products and the PPA portfolio for 2027.
    answered directly
  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: Can you clarify the drivers behind the change in the tariff expectation to $1.1 billion and provide an update on the excavator launch and broader adoption?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: The tariff change from $1.2 billion to $1.1 billion is due to Section 232 tariff reductions from 25% to 15% effective June 1st, and three models of Deere-designed excavators launched this spring with rollouts planned over the next three to four years.
    answered directly
  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did anything slip between Q3 and Q4, and why does tractor inventory look higher relative to sales ahead of the industry?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Shipment plans are set with orders on hand, pulling some demand ahead into Q4, which is expected to match Q3 net sales for PPA and C&F though margins will be slightly lower from seasonal R&D, SA&G loads, and lack of refunds.
    answered partially
  • Tim Thein, Raymond James: How should we think about the potential impact from higher technology sales on mix in 2027 given current market conditions?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: It is too early to determine the exact mix impact for 2027 given ongoing agricultural volatility, shipment disruptions, and softness in Europe and Brazil, though EOP signals and technology adoption trends are encouraging.
    answered partially
  • Jerry Revich, Wells Fargo: Can you unpack the demand variability seen across regions in the early order program and discuss moving pieces toward a potential high single-digit range?
    Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division, Production & Precision Ag, Sales and Marketing, Regions of the Americas and Australia: U.S. EOPs are trending slightly better than Canada with early orders up mid-single digits, supported by technology take rates including a doubling of See & Spray and 40% advanced planter adoption.

Deere reported fiscal Q3 2026 net sales and revenues of $12.608 billion, up 5%, with net income of $1.379 billion. Early order programs showed mid-single-digit growth, reinforcing the company's view that 2026 represents the bottom of the agricultural equipment cycle. Technology adoption accelerated, with strong take rates for advanced features on planters and sprayers.

Key takeaways

  • Deanna Kovar, President, Worldwide Agriculture & Turf Division, Production & Precision Ag, Sales and Marketing, Regions of the Americas and Australia, noted that 40% of planters are taking advanced technologies and See & Spray orders on factory-installed sprayers have doubled.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, stated the company expects modest underproduction this year for Production & Precision Ag and Construction & Forestry to manage inventory.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, noted that the FTC settlement formalizes tools like John Deere Operations Center PRO Service to support customers maintaining their own equipment.
  • Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations, stated that 30% to 35% of earthmoving transactions start as a rental, presenting an opportunity to grow dealer-owned rental fleets.

Key metrics

  • Net sales and revenues $12.608 billion up 5%
  • Net income attributable to Deere & Company $1.379 billion N/A

  • Small Ag & Turf net sales $3.383 billion up 12%
  • Construction & Forestry net sales $3.618 billion up 18%
  • Total tariff refunds recognized in FY26 $382 million N/A
  • Equipment operations operating margin 14.4% N/A

Financial highlights

  • Net sales and revenues reached $12.608 billion, up 5%.
  • Net income attributable to Deere & Company was $1.379 billion, with diluted EPS of $5.10.

Q&A

  • Tami Zakaria, JPMorgan: What is driving the tariff expectation change to $1.1 billion and could you give updates on the excavator launch?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: The change from $1.2 billion to $1.1 billion is mainly attributed to changes in Section 232 tariffs, which dropped from 25% to 15% effective June 1, and the first Deere-designed excavators launched this spring with very positive feedback.
    answered directly
  • Kristen Owen, Oppenheimer: Did something slip between 3Q and 4Q regarding inventory, as it looks like you built some tractor inventory in 3Q ahead of the industry?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Demand was pulled ahead to manage risk in Q4, but factories are hitting forecasts and retail pace across the Americas is on trend, with Q4 margins expected to be slightly lower due to higher R&D, SA&G, and the absence of refunds.

  • Steve Volkmann, Jefferies: How are the C&F early order programs shaping up, and how much of that is dealer rental fleet loading?
    Chris Seibert, Director of Investor Relations: Order trends for C&F have been very positive with about four to five months of orders on hand, driven by large infrastructure projects, data center starts, independent rental channels, and dealer-owned rental fleets.

    Brent Norwood, Chief Financial Officer: Net tariffs will be a headwind going into next year, with a run rate closer to $1 billion compared to a net exposure of approximately $750 million this year after $382 million in refunds.

Inputs are the latest transcript for each ticker (and the one before it) from the defeatbeta dataset, and live Yahoo profiles, pulled 2026-10-11. Each model was called once per input, so a model's score on a single ticker has noise; the averages across six calls and twelve tickers are the signal. Prices are list prices checked on October 11, 2026; Gemini Flash prices double on January 1, 2027.

Reproduce: node scripts/bakeoff/llm-bakeoff.mjs inputs|gen|gen2|check|judge (gen2 haiku-s,sonnet-s,opus-s,fable-s writes prompt files for subagents under scripts/bakeoff/sub/), node scripts/bakeoff/ladder.mjs, then node scripts/bakeoff/bakeoff-report.mjs.
